When the previous round's claims all held, nothing has\r\nbeen added or repaired since, and the only motive is that an auditor could still imagine an attack,\r\nthere is no subject �?\" closing is the correct action and the next subject is the deliverable.\r\n\r\nWhen the same class has recurred along one seam to the budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md`\r\nA\u0015 Rounds and verdicts, the next move is that law's strategy switch �?\" the breadth sweep of the\r\nstream, the ruling, or the dropped moving-target claim �?\" rather than another successor round.\r\n\r\n## Write the brief\r\n\r\nThe brief is the instrument. A weak brief produces a confirming review no matter which auditor reads\r\nit. Follow `references/brief.md`. What this skill adds beyond the conduct law:\r\n\r\n- Every re-run is a **successor**, not a restatement. It carries what the previous round closed so\r\n  nothing is re-reported, and it adds claims that attack **the previous round's own rulings**.\r\n- The brief names what the round **decides**. An auditor that does not know the stakes calibrates to\r\n  politeness.\r\n- Unknowns are named as unknowns, with how the auditor reports back on them.\r\n\r\nThe claim form itself is the Falsification law's �?\" read it there. The **verdict shape** below is this\r\nskill's, because `.agents/orchestration.md` assigns it here; everything else about auditor conduct is the law's.\r\n\r\n## Evidence, by subject type\r\n\r\nThe brief supplies the evidence its subject actually has. Requiring a diff for a subject that has no\r\ndiff is a rule the brief cannot satisfy.\r\n\r\n| subject                                 | required evidence                                                                     |\r\n| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| a code change                           | the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch deviation |\r\n| a rendered or externally driven surface | the capture portfolio as primary, source as corroboration                             |\r\n| a policy, design, or process proposal   | the proposal, the canon it must satisfy, and the record of what motivated it          |\r\n\r\n**A subject can occupy more than one row; supply every row it occupies.** A ruling whose fixes\r\nalready landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change, and withholding the diff on the grounds\r\nthat the subject is \"a proposal\" leaves the auditor unable to check whether a fix changed anything\r\nnobody claimed.\r\n\r\n## Run the round\r\n\r\n- **Verify every authority the brief references exists in the tree the auditor is rooted in.** A\r\n  brief that points at a rule file, section, or guide the executor cannot find delivers nothing while\r\n  looking like authority �?\" and it fails silently, because an auditor does not report a heading it\r\n  never saw. Check before dispatch; propagate the missing file rather than restating its contents in\r\n  the brief. This is the reason restatement felt necessary, and it is the wrong cure. Where the\r\n  executor's tree carries a superseded vendored copy of an authority, take the stale-authority\r\n  branch in `references/brief.md` A\u0015 \"What not to put in a brief\".\r\n- Run the **adversarial pass** on one identical brief: a subjective lane and an objective\r\n  lane, each a fresh subagent with a clean context, blind to each other. Reconcile them yourself.\r\n  `.agents/orchestration.md` owns lane definitions, engine assignment, and what happens when an\r\n  engine is dark; do not restate them here.\r\n- `.agents/orchestration.md` A\u0015 Execution loop owns which lanes a round runs and the deviation a\r\n  short round records; A\u0015 Engine assignment owns the substitution.\r\n- **Pair every finder with an independent refuter when the round fans out past the subjective and\r\n  objective lanes.** The\r\n  refuter receives one slice's findings, never that finder's work, and is briefed to BREAK them\r\n  rather than to re-audit the subject. It reproduces each stated vector itself and defaults to\r\n  refuted when uncertain.\r\n- Refute on any of these grounds, and name which: the vector does not reproduce; the behaviour is\r\n  correct and documented; it is unreachable through the public API or a documented seam; it asks\r\n  for new capability rather than naming a defect; it restates a finding an earlier round\r\n  repaired; or its diagnosis is wrong �?\" then CONFIRM with the correction.\r\n- Only a survivor earns a fix unit. An unrefuted finding is a hypothesis. The scope grounds,\r\n  unreachable and new capability, are what keep a round from drifting into a redesign.\r\n- **Give every auditor the means to run its attacks.** A lens that can only read returns derivations,\r\n  and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict �?\" it will confirm a claim that one probe would break.\r\n- **Tell each auditor exactly where a probe may live, and verify that place works before you say it.**\r\n  A test runner resolves only what its own configuration includes: a probe written outside every\r\n  configured project is not discovered at all, and the run reports no test files rather than a result.\r\n  Where the repository provides a probe project, use it and name that project in the brief;\r\n  `.claude/rules/tests.md` governs what may live there. Where it does not, the reliable form is a file\r\n  inside the canonical mirrored suite, run by explicit path. Either way the probe is deleted before the\r\n  auditor returns, and promoted into a permanent test when it proves something worth keeping. Give each\r\n  concurrent auditor a distinct filename that already satisfies the repository's test naming\r\n  convention; never invent a prefix to dodge collisions, and never let two auditors claim one path. A\r\n  probe left in the mirrored suite is discovered and fails a run nobody else caused.\r\n- **Run auditors concurrently only when their writes cannot collide.** A lens that can execute\r\n  still writes probes; give each a distinct path and forbid whole-project runs, or serialize the\r\n  round. **This binds\r\n  the orchestrator too:** a tree-wide gate run while a round is live sees the auditors' in-flight probes\r\n  and reports a failure nobody caused. Wait for the round, or scope the command to paths no auditor\r\n  owns. Never delete another executor's working file to make your own command pass.\r\n- **Tell a lane when its own engine wrote the half it is auditing, and tell it to attack that half\r\n  harder.** A fix round reviewed by the engine that wrote it is the case the round exists to avoid,\r\n  and where the pass cannot avoid it, naming it is what recovers the round. A clean pass on its own\r\n  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\r\n- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\r\n- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Read-only describes the SUBJECT, never the lane's\r\n  tools.\r\n- **Read the lane's allowlist before writing its brief.** A lane with no write tool cannot create\r\n  a probe; a lane with no exec tool cannot run one or read `git`. Naming either stops the unit on\r\n  arrival over a detail the allowlist already settled.\r\n- Where the lane cannot execute, run the probe yourself, record its control and its output, and\r\n  supply that record as the lane's evidence. The Orchestrator produces, the lane rules. Never\r\n  widen a lane's tools to fit a brief.\r\n- Blind reports are **immutable**. Nothing an auditor returns is edited, merged, or revised �?\" by\r\n  anyone, including the auditor �?\" once it has been returned.\r\n\r\n## Verdict shape\r\n\r\nEvery auditor in every round returns exactly this, and nothing else. One shape makes rounds\r\ncomparable; a round that invents its own cannot be read against the last one.\r\n\r\n1. **Numbered verdicts**, one per claim, in the brief's order. Exactly one of these values:\r\n\r\n   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\r\n   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\r\n   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires �?\" note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\r\n   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it; a claim whose only evidence is the writer's own report takes this value                                                                 |\r\n   | `NOT-EVIDENCED` | a claim about a rendered or externally driven surface the supplied capture cannot show | which capture is missing                                                                                                                                      |\r\n\r\n   `CONFIRMED` and `BROKEN` defer; only `UNRESOLVED` and `NOT-EVIDENCED` are this skill's, because\r\n   the law does not name them. `BROKEN` and `UNRESOLVED` are **separate**: a claim nobody could\r\n   decide has not been falsified, and it cannot supply the fields falsification requires.\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED` is the auditing lanes' own token; it is kept, not re-invented.\r\n\r\n2. **Findings fitting no claim**, if any, each substantiated to the same standard as `BROKEN`.\r\n\r\n3. **Attacked and held**: the claims this round attacked and could not break, each with the attack\r\n   that failed, and the adjacent behaviour that looks like the defect and is correct. This is where\r\n   the next round reads what has already been tried, so a claim listed here without its attack is\r\n   worth nothing to it. A claim's own `CONFIRMED` line already carries the evidence that convinced\r\n   the auditor, so list here only the attacks no verdict line carries and the adjacent behaviour.\r\n\r\n4. **One terminal line**, and only one:\r\n\r\n   ```text\r\n   VERDICT: PASS\r\n   VERDICT: FAIL <claim numbers>; outside the claims: <finding ids>\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n   The claim numbers are every claim that is not `CONFIRMED`. Write `none` in a slot the round\r\n   leaves empty, so a `FAIL` driven only by a finding outside the claims still names both.\r\n\r\n   **`PASS` requires all of this**: every claim `CONFIRMED`, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED`, and no substantiated finding outside the claims. A single substantiated finding\r\n   forces `FAIL` however the numbered claims landed �?\" otherwise a round can report a real\r\n   defect and still emit the word that authorises the release.\r\n\r\nNo process diary. No summary of what was read.\r\n\r\n## When a round comes back all-confirmed\r\n\r\nThe Falsification law governs this: the brief goes on trial, not the subject. Apply it, and if the\r\nclaims turn out to have been descriptive, the successor brief is where the sharper ones go.\r\n\r\n## Reconcile and rule\r\n\r\nFollow `references/reconcile.md`. The obligations that are not delegable:\r\n\r\n- **Reproduce every sharp finding yourself** before acting on it. An auditor's finding is a\r\n  hypothesis until the orchestrator has run it.\r\n- **Build before you pack.** `npm pack` runs no build, so it ships whatever `dist/` is on disk. An\r\n  auditor who packs an artifact to inspect it is reading the last build, not the current source, and\r\n  will report deleted exports as still shipping. Run the package's build first, and say in the brief\r\n  that the tarball was built from the commit under audit.\r\n- **Check the subject before acting on a finding, not only the reasoning.** A brief that names attack\r\n  vectors teaches the lane those vectors matter, and a lane can hand back the brief's own questions as\r\n  the subject's claims �?\" demanding coverage for a property the subject never documented. Grep the\r\n  subject for the claim the finding rests on. Where it is not there, the finding is against the brief.\r\n- **A disagreement between auditors is rarely a tie to average.** It is usually correct answers\r\n  to different questions. Find the question each one answered.\r\n- **Bound every finding**: state what is _not_ broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks the\r\n  same is correct. An audit that reports everything is as useless as one that reports nothing.\r\n- **Bound the fix before briefing it.** Establish what over-correcting would break, and include that\r\n  in the fix brief as a constraint.\r\n- Drop, on the record, any finding neither auditor can substantiate.\r\n\r\n## Accept, or run it again\r\n\r\nThe threshold is **a `PASS` terminal line on a brief whose claims cover what the subject owns** �?\"\r\nevery numbered claim `CONFIRMED` on evidence, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing `NOT-EVIDENCED`, no\r\nsubstantiated finding beside them. Never green gates, which prove only that a suite ran.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is a success, not a delay. The alternative is a consumer finding it\r\nafter publication, when the version number is already spent. But an unsubstantiated attack is not a\r\nfinding, and the supply of imaginable ones never runs out: a round is not re-run because an auditor\r\ncan still think of one. A substantiated finding against something no claim names is real and forces\r\n`FAIL`; an unsubstantiated one is a claim for the successor brief. The escalation law in\r\n`.claude/rules/quality.md` governs a subject that keeps producing findings at the same seam �?\" after\r\nenough of them the ruling owed is on the design, not on the next defect.\r\n\r\nWhen a fix round follows, its auditor must be an engine that did not write it, and the next round's\r\nbrief is the successor of this one.\r\n# Reconciling a round and ruling on it\r\n\r\nThe auditors return. Neither accepts; the orchestrator does. This is where a round becomes a\r\ndecision, and it is not delegable.\r\n\r\n## Reproduce before you act\r\n\r\nThe rule beneath this whole section: **run it rather than argue it.** Every judgement below is\r\ncheap once the probe exists and unreliable until it does.\r\n\r\nAn auditor's finding is a **hypothesis** until the orchestrator has run it. Reproduce every sharp\r\nclaim by hand, against the built output, before it enters a fix brief.\r\n\r\n**Build the hostile input outside the `try`.** A probe that wraps construction and invocation in one\r\ncatch cannot distinguish _the subject threw_ from _my harness threw_ �?\" a missing import, a wrong\r\narity, a `require` in an ESM context all surface as the finding you were hoping to see. Construct\r\nfirst, let harness failures crash loudly, and only guard the call under test.\r\n\r\nReproduction produces these outcomes, and all of them matter:\r\n\r\n- the finding **confirms** and is often **wider** than reported �?\" the reproduction reaches doors the\r\n  auditor did not try;\r\n- the finding **confirms but is bounded smaller** �?\" real, and not where the auditor thought;\r\n- the finding **evaporates**, because the auditor's input could not exercise what it claimed to test.\r\n\r\nSeparate a dead finding from a dead vector before evaporating anything. A reported vector the\r\ncompiler rejects refutes the vector alone; re-derive one the types admit, and record which vector\r\nwas actually tested.\r\n\r\nThe same reproduction discipline applies to your own probes. A probe whose input cannot reach the\r\ncode under test reports a pass that means nothing, and it will read exactly like a real pass.\r\n\r\n## A disagreement is rarely a tie\r\n\r\nWhen auditors return opposite verdicts on one claim, do not average them and do not prefer the\r\nengine you trust more. **Reproduce first** �?\" running the disagreement settles most of them outright,\r\nand it is the only method that can also find what neither auditor saw. Then find the question each\r\none answered. The common shapes:\r\n\r\n- **Both right about different objects.** One tested a case the other did not construct. This is a\r\n  `SPLIT-CLAIM`: the claim was a universal that carried more than one subject. It is **not** a\r\n  further verdict value �?\" one falsifying input makes a universal claim `BROKEN`, and succeeding on a\r\n  different object does not undo that. Split it, keep the original `BROKEN` if any subclaim is\r\n  broken, and carry the split into the successor brief.\r\n- **Both right about different halves of one claim number.** Same resolution: the claim number was\r\n  carrying more than one claim. Split and renumber.\r\n- **One right on the mechanism, the other on the criterion.** Take both. The reconciled ruling is\r\n  frequently neither proposal, and better than either, because each supplied a constraint the other\r\n  violated.\r\n- **The consequence disproves the premise.** An argument that an input class is unreachable is\r\n  answered by running it and showing what the reachable consequence is.\r\n\r\nRecord which engine was right and on what. A round whose disagreements are smoothed over teaches\r\nnothing to the next one.\r\n\r\n## Evidence custody\r\n\r\nBlind reports are immutable, and their independence is a property of the record, not of anyone's\r\nmemory. A reader six months out must be able to tell an unbiased blind verdict from one produced\r\nafter an auditor saw its counterpart's evidence �?\" otherwise the whole value of running blind is\r\nunverifiable after the fact.\r\n\r\nThese rules are enforceable:\r\n\r\n1. **A returned verdict is never edited** �?\" not by the auditor, not by the orchestrator.\r\n2. **Anything an auditor says after seeing another's report is a separate file beside that verdict**,\r\n   under `.orkestrel/<package>/`, named for the unit and the exposure, recording what was shown and\r\n   to whom. It is a durable record, not a journal, so it survives the campaign sweep.\r\n\r\nThat exchange is a **fallback, not a phase.** Reproduction comes first and settles most\r\ndisagreements. Reach for an exchange only when a specific factual question survives reproduction,\r\nscope it to that question, initiate it yourself, and run it once �?\" a second exchange is negotiation.\r\n\r\n**Ask the auditor to attack the other's evidence on that question. Never ask it to resolve the\r\ndisagreement, reconsider its position, or say whether the other changed its mind.** Constraining\r\nwhen, who, scope and frequency does nothing about convergence if the instruction itself invites it,\r\nand \"does their evidence change your claim?\" is the convergence prompt in its purest form.\r\n\r\n## Bound the finding\r\n\r\nState what is **not** broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks identical is correct. A\r\nfinding without a boundary is an alarm, and alarms get discounted wholesale �?\" including the true\r\nones next to them.\r\n\r\nThese boundaries earn their keep:\r\n\r\n- **Credit what the round got right.** If the hostile inputs adjacent to the hole are correctly\r\n  contained, say so and list them. It sharpens the finding to a point instead of an area.\r\n- **Show where the same-looking answer is correct.** When several exports answer a hostile input the\r\n  same way and only one is wrong, name what makes the difference �?\" usually that the correct ones\r\n  agree with a documented view, and the wrong one reads on an axis it then ignores.\r\n\r\n## Bound the fix before briefing it\r\n\r\nEstablish what over-correcting would break, and put it in the fix brief as a constraint. Both ends\r\nare usually wrong:\r\n\r\n- **too little** �?\" a patch to the one function, leaving the package holding conflicting standards\r\n  for the same thing, which is the inconsistency that produced the finding;\r\n- **too much** �?\" adopting the strictest sibling's rule verbatim, breaking a legitimate caller\r\n  pattern, and tripping \"no refusal was widened into a regression\" in the next round.\r\n\r\nFind the rule that fits both. It is usually about **agreement** rather than about categories �?\" what\r\na reader reads, its answer must carry �?\" and it dissolves the special cases rather than enumerating\r\nthem.\r\n\r\nMeasure a proposed fix before adopting it; it is itself a claim. Run it against the set it must\r\nnot break, including every case an earlier round pinned. Where it fails that set, document the\r\nlimit on the helper that owns it and pin the limit with a test that names it as one. A heuristic\r\nthat trades one wrong answer for another fails quietly; a stated boundary does not.\r\n\r\nWhere the choice is genuinely open, it is a design judgement with a subjective and an objective\r\nhalf, and it goes to a blind design pass before code. Ruling it unilaterally is how a fix round\r\nbecomes the next audit's finding.\r\n\r\n## Certifying an instrument\r\n\r\nThe control-population law in `.claude/rules/quality.md` binds here without restatement. What it\r\nleaves this round is the procedure.\r\n\r\nWhen a round certifies an instrument �?\" a pin, an identity check, a generated sweep �?\" the controls\r\nare usually drawn from whatever the instrument obviously covers, because that is where the examples\r\ntake the least construction. That sampling proves discrimination _within_ the population and is\r\nroutinely reported as proof the instrument works.\r\n\r\nSo before running controls, write down the instrument's **membership rule** in one sentence, then\r\nask what the rule excludes. Draw at least one control from there. These shapes have already cost a\r\nround each:\r\n\r\n- an AST comparison whose controls were all drawn from the literal classes present in the bodies it\r\n  guarded, blind to the classes absent from them;\r\n- a call-closure pin whose controls were all body-reachable functions, green for a function reached\r\n  only through a parameter default.\r\n\r\nThen write the sentences that matter: what the controls established, and what they did not. What\r\nthey did not establish is the one that gets skipped, and skipping it is how an instrument's\r\ncredibility outruns its evidence.\r\n\r\n## Ruling\r\n\r\n- Every retained finding names the fix-brief item that carries it. A finding with no carrier is a\r\n  dropped finding; walk the list once and check.\r\n- Drop, **on the record**, anything neither auditor can substantiate against the evidence.\r\n- Promote anything that must outlive the round into a durable artifact before the working files are\r\n  swept. What lives only in a scratch file did not survive.\r\n- The fix round's auditor is an engine that did not write it.\r\n- The next round's brief is this round's successor.\r\n\r\n## The threshold\r\n\r\nAccept when the brief's claims are **satisfied on evidence** �?\" the `PASS` terminal line the skill\r\ndefines, against a claim set that covers what the subject owns. Not green gates.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is the process working. A round that finds nothing because nobody tried\r\nis the failure; a round re-run because an attack can still be imagined never ends. Bound the claim\r\nset at the brief, rule on what it returned, and close.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*'\r\n  - 'app/**/*'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'tsconfig.json'\r\n  - '.agents/skills/**/*'\r\n  - '.claude/skills/**/*'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Research, hardening, and completion rules\r\n\r\n## Evidence before change\r\n\r\n- Research when the user asks for it, when comparing an upstream, protocol, or legacy implementation, or when current external behavior materially affects design.\r\n- Use current primary sources for external capabilities, and the exact installed declarations and guides for dependencies. Separate verified fact from inference.\r\n- Read authoritative types and named decision-bearing implementation files first-hand. Delegate bulk supporting context, never the owning design decision.\r\n- Treat existing code, tests, `old/`, branches, and copied projects as evidence, not authority.\r\n- Build a capability/defect matrix before a broad API or production-readiness change. Every row ends as implement, repair, retain, or intentionally exclude with evidence.\r\n- Fix that matrix when the change starts. It is the definition of done, and the change closes when every row closes.\r\n- Never end a row as \"hardened further.\" Replace any evaluative phrase with the concrete condition that closes the row.\r\n- Record a finding outside the matrix against the row that owns it, for the next matrix. Do not reopen this one.\r\n\r\n## Probes before arguments\r\n\r\nA question a probe can settle is settled by the probe, whether or not anyone has disputed it. Reasoning decides what to run and what the result means. It does not replace the run.\r\n\r\n- Test your own assumptions before you rely on them. You do not need a disagreement to justify a probe. If you are about to depend on what a function returns, what a config resolves to, what a flag does, or whether a path is even reached, run it and find out.\r\n- Treat a long deliberation about behaviour as the signal to stop and run something. Deliberation that a ten-line probe would have ended is the most expensive habit in this process, and it is invisible because it feels like rigour.\r\n- Verify a belief before stating it, or label it as unverified. An unverified assertion put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream, including other agents, and correcting it later costs more than the check would have.\r\n- Hold a claim you issue to the standard you hold a claim you receive. Nothing downstream re-checks an instruction, so an unverified fact inside one becomes the premise of every task built on it. Auditing every report you receive while exempting every brief you write is the common form of this failure.\r\n- Prefer the check over the chain of reasoning whenever the check is cheap. Reading the installed declaration, running one line, or counting the call sites beats three paragraphs inferring the same thing, and it produces evidence you can hand to someone else.\r\n- Write the probe before the argument gets long. The moment a claim about behaviour is disputed �?\" between engines, between a report and a rule, or in your own head �?\" stop and run it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output.\r\n- Bound the search before starting it, and put the bound in the brief. Name the benchmark, the population, or the row that ends it. An investigation with no stated stopping condition runs until attention runs out. This includes the case where the honest answer is that the limit is inherent and belongs in documentation rather than in code.\r\n- Treat a negative probe as evidence about the probe until its input is shown to reach the code under test. A pass proves nothing if the vector never arrived. Instrument the path, assert an observable side effect, or drive it through a door you can see.\r\n- When a report names a defect and your reproduction comes back clean, assume first that your vector was weaker than theirs, and go get their exact vector. Treating a failed reproduction as a disproof is the most common way a true finding is lost.\r\n- A reported vector the compiler rejects refutes the VECTOR, never the finding. Where a claim asserts reachability through a typed API, compile the exact vector under the project's own settings; re-derive one the types admit before dropping the finding, and record which vector was actually tested.\r\n- Reproduce a reported defect's cause before instructing a fix from it. A real symptom can carry a wrong diagnosis, and a fix aimed at the stated cause edits the wrong file while the defect survives.\r\n- Do not read a result into a tool's failure to find your probe. \"No tests found\", an empty match, a skipped file, a runner that resolved nothing �?\" these report on the harness, not the subject. Confirm the probe was collected and executed first.\r\n- Prefer an observation over a derivation, including your own. When a measurement and an argument disagree, the argument is wrong until the measurement is shown to be broken.\r\n- Diagnose from the artifact the work produces �?\" the file, the count, the exit code, the timestamp on what changed �?\" never from a wrapper or a proxy signal that merely correlates with progress. Read a gate bare: a pipeline stage after it (`| tail`, `| grep`) reports the stage's exit status and hides the gate's failing lines.\r\n- Verify a comment or an agent's report against the call sites before relying on it. A code comment is not evidence. When lanes disagree about whether a path is live, count the callers rather than weighing the prose.\r\n- What a round proves is what it ran. A conclusion carried from one door to another is a hypothesis at the second door. Re-run it there.\r\n\r\n## Falsification\r\n\r\nA review that reads a diff finds what the diff shows. A review that tries to break named claims finds what the diff hides. Code that has passed diff review several times can still carry a defect nobody has tried to trigger.\r\n\r\n### Writing the claims\r\n\r\n- State the subject as a numbered list of the claims the work makes, never as \"review this diff.\" Each claim is falsifiable: a property some concrete input, state, or interleaving could show false.\r\n- Instruct the auditor to attempt refutation, not confirmation. A claim it cannot break is CONFIRMED with the evidence that convinced it. A claim it breaks is BROKEN with the exact failing input, state, or interleaving, plus the smallest correct fix.\r\n- Derive claims from what the change asserts under adverse conditions: cancellation, restart, concurrency, partial failure, hostile input, resource exhaustion, and the orderings a happy path never reaches.\r\n- Read the installed declaration or implementation of every substrate a claim depends on. A claim about `stop()` is unfalsifiable until you know what `stop()` does when the status is not the one the caller assumed.\r\n\r\n### Instruments\r\n\r\n- An instrument is not evidence until it has failed. Pair every probe, comparison, or matrix with a negative control that must report failure, run under the same conditions. An identity check whose control reports \"same\" has measured nothing.\r\n- When a question about a TypeScript edit can supply a workspace project, a case of workspace files with a test, and a negative control naming its files, its test, the stage it must fail at, and why, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the answer. When the question supplies no project, no case, or no control, follow `.claude/rules/tests.md` A\u0015 Probes and report the fallback instrument's own control and coverage.\r\n- When no `probe` server is registered in the session, register one outside the repository, in the harness's own local or user MCP scope �?\" in Claude Code, `claude mcp add` outside project scope �?\" naming the installed `node_modules/@orkestrel/probe/dist/bin/main.js` entry, and start it in the repository whose projects the question names, because the server fixes its workspace from its own working directory. The registration cannot live in the tree: a scaffold target holds no `.mcp.json` file, that path is instruction canon, and a copy at it reports as foreign drift on every `scaffold audit` run. Where the harness registers no server at all, treat the question as supplying no project and take the preceding rule's fallback.\r\n- Quote the closing line of the `prove` answer verbatim in every report, brief, and audit verdict that rests on the claim: the `receipt probe:<digest>:�?�` line when the case ran clean and the control broke exactly where the claim declared it would, and the `no receipt` line otherwise. A `no receipt` line leaves the claim unproved �?\" report it with the stage that refused.\r\n- Read a receipt as evidence about its claim, never as a gate result. The gate chain still runs, and `verifier` still owns its result.\r\n- Draw the negative control from outside the population the instrument covers. Name the instrument's membership rule first, then pick a control that rule excludes. A control sampled from constructs the instrument already handles proves only that it discriminates among those constructs, and says nothing about the class it silently cannot reach.\r\n- State an instrument's coverage beside its result. A conclusion inherits the instrument's scope, not the question's. An unstated coverage claim is read as complete, and it never is. A search proves something about the paths it walked, so name them.\r\n- Match the instrument to the question. A text search reports on text, so a claim about declarations, call sites, or structure needs the compiler or a parser instead. A pattern written for one spelling of a construct reports on that spelling alone. A path check answers relative to the directory it runs from, so resolve the inputs against their own base before reading a miss as a finding.\r\n- Name the rival reading the instrument must exclude, and show it reports differently under that reading. Give independent measurements independent state: one counter shared across members reports read order and per-member read count identically, so a result consistent with both measured neither.\r\n- Report a question unanswered rather than answering it with a weaker instrument. A fallback that measures something adjacent returns a confident wrong answer, and nothing downstream can tell that answer from the real one �?\" searching commit messages for a release when the question is where a version changed will match some release, but not the one asked about. Name the substitute and what it actually measures, or say the question is open.\r\n- State what the controls established and what they did not. An instrument certified only from the inside is trusted exactly where it has never been tested.\r\n- Treat a gap between what an instrument says it checks and what it actually matches as a defect in the instrument, not as a documented limit. A recorded blind spot buys trust only when everything outside it is genuinely covered.\r\n- Measure the product, not the harness. A recorded baseline that counts something about its own fixture is not evidence about the shipped surface, however often a guide quotes it.\r\n- Baseline a published-artifact claim against the published artifact. \"Did my change move the surface\" and \"does this release differ from the last one\" are different questions, and a diff against your own starting point answers only the first. A toolchain that re-emits declarations moves the artifact without any source edit, so every writer can correctly report an unmoved surface while the package's published contract has changed. Fetch what consumers actually have �?\" the tarball, the deployed asset �?\" and compare against that.\r\n- Prove a module cycle by loading the built artifact, not by a green suite. Tests import through the source graph and a bundler resolves it differently, so a cycle that is fatal at module-init in the shipped form can stay invisible under every test. Import each published entry point and read an export from it.\r\n- Adopt an instrument that settled a claim as a test before accepting the work it settled. The probe that proved a fix, carrying the control that proved the probe, is that fix's regression guard. A verification that runs once is a rehearsal, not a gate.\r\n\r\n### Rounds and verdicts\r\n\r\n- Treat an all-confirmed round as a legitimate result, and put the brief on trial rather than the subject. Re-read the claims and ask whether any could have been falsified by evidence the round actually had. If none could, the claims were descriptive and the round proved nothing �?\" sharpen them and re-run. If they could have been and were not, the pass stands.\r\n- Name the claims you could not break either way, so the next round knows what has already been attacked.\r\n- Never tell an auditor that a clean round means it did not try. That instructs it to manufacture a finding, and a manufactured finding costs a fix unit, an argument, and the credibility of the true findings beside it.\r\n- Treat a repaired claim as a new claim, not a settled one. Re-ask it at every entry point that reaches the same rule, not only the door the defect arrived through. The engine that wrote the fix is least able to see this, because re-verifying where the fix is feels like verifying the fix.\r\n- A fix that adopts the auditor's prescription verbatim may close with a mutation probe in place of a fresh audit round: disable the load-bearing line, watch the adopted pin fail, restore it, and commit the pin as the regression guard. A fix that departs from the prescription gets the cross-engine round.\r\n- Let reachability bound the fix. A defect reachable through the package's own shipped code or a documented extension seam falsifies its claim and is repaired now.\r\n- Document the obligation instead when a defect is reachable only through a hypothetical foreign implementation of a contract this package publishes. State it on the interface that owns it and prove the documentation. Do not build coordination machinery against a requirement nobody wrote down. Attacks are unlimited; reachable ones are not, and only the reachable set is a work list.\r\n- **Three rounds at one seam is the budget, and reaching it switches the search strategy rather than stopping the work.** Repeated rounds against one seam are evidence about the design, not evidence of diligence. At the third round, name what the audit is trying to accomplish, then pick the successor strategy from what the recurrence shows.\r\n- Recurrence with a direction �?\" each fix relocates the class along one stream, or the evidence points up a dependency chain �?\" ends the depth search. Run one breadth round that probes the stream's stations in parallel to locate the source, in the shape `.agents/orchestration.md` A\u0015 Context and decomposition prescribes, then plan the downstream work from the source and size it by the sweep's map.\r\n- Recurrence with no direction makes the seam itself the question. The next unit is a ruling �?\" on the threat model, the mechanism, or the boundary �?\" taken with the same adversarial pass a design gets, not a fourth repair.\r\n- A subject that reprices itself on every edit �?\" a count, a census, a total over prose �?\" has no closing condition and is not a seam. Drop the claim, or recast it as the property the tally stood in for.\r\n- Write the round count down in the capability/defect matrix row that owns the seam, when the seam opens, so it is a fact rather than a feeling. A seam that has consumed more rounds than the rest of the matrix combined has already answered the question.\r\n- State the ruling that ends a seam as the invariant the code will obey, the constraint bounding it against over-correction, and the interface where a consumer meets the obligation. A ruling that names only the defect it replaces produces the opposite defect next round.\r\n- Give every behavioural audit the means to run its attacks. An auditor that cannot execute cannot falsify a behavioural claim: it returns derivations, and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict while being a different thing �?\" it will confirm a claim one probe would break. Treat a report with no executed evidence as a review of the source, and label it as such.\r\n\r\n## Ecosystem reuse\r\n\r\nThe root laws on inspecting declared `@orkestrel/*` capabilities, reusing a matching primitive, and updating every consumer without shims bind here without restatement. This file adds the judgment calls:\r\n\r\n- Prove the semantic difference before keeping a local variant. Similar names are not evidence of different behavior.\r\n- Treat downstream friction as valid evidence of a reusable upstream defect, not as automatic proof. Fix the lowest package that owns the general mechanism and keep product policy downstream.\r\n- Never re-export a dependency's symbol to soften a consumer's import.\r\n\r\n## Production hardening\r\n\r\n- Translate \"enterprise-grade\" or \"production-ready\" into an explicit risk and seam matrix covering applicable inputs, states, failures, cleanup, cancellation, concurrency, resource ownership, hostile boundaries, environment isolation, serialization and restore, and package consumption.\r\n- Grade that matrix on coverage, not optimization: whether every applicable seam exists, works, and stays proven. Do not grade on how many further interleavings can be invented against the one seam already proven. A surface polished past its row buys less than the next uncovered seam would have.\r\n- Test observable invariants at each applicable seam with real implementations.\r\n- Use dedicated real-service projects for external model or service behavior. Require readiness and tune each request to the smallest proof that still exercises the claim.\r\n- Audit test discovery, counts, skipped and todo tests, cleanup, and assertion adequacy. Passing discovered tests alone is insufficient.\r\n- Inspect public exports, declarations, supported runtime targets, and generated outputs.\r\n- Treat a claim that a surface works with an external client as unproven until one representative real client of that class has driven it end to end. Protocol tests prove the protocol, not the integration.\r\n- Add an independent adversarial review for security, destructive paths, concurrency, protocols, or untrusted external input.\r\n\r\n## Completion\r\n\r\nThe root completion law �?\" finish every in-scope capability now, leave no TODO, deferral, or hidden follow-up, and run the applicable repository skill for comprehensive work �?\" binds here without restatement. Stopping there is correct, and the next scope is the deliverable. A further pass over the same surface requires a new instruction from the user, not an auditor's remaining appetite. Depth is owed to what the scope names, not to what an engine can still imagine about it.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

A `Conversation` holds the history �?\" a live tail of immutable messages plus the sections older turns were compacted into. An `AgentContext` assembles that history into the next prompt, folding in the instructions and the active workspace and applying the active scope. An `Agent` drives that prompt through a provider, dispatches whatever tools the model asks for, feeds the results back, and repeats until the model stops. Everything else in this module either configures those nouns or observes them. Source: [`src/core`](../src/core). Published through `@orkestrel/agent`.\r\n\r\nHand a provider a conversation and get back one assembled `ProviderResult` (`generate`), or a live stream of channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s that returns that same assembled result when it ends (`stream`). Reasoning separation, the authority gate, and durable jobs sit around that boundary. The model itself is the one thing this package does not supply. `ProviderInterface` stays the boundary any backend can satisfy, and `AgentProvider` is the host-independent HTTP engine a concrete provider extends rather than rewrites: it owns the deadline, the transport, the bounded error read, the framing loop, reasoning separation, and result assembly, and the subclass fills in one vendor's wire. `RelayProvider` and `createRelay` carry that same boundary across your own server, so a browser drives a model it holds no credential for. There is no hidden global state, a plugin lifecycle, a prompt-template DSL, or an implicit memory store. This is a kit of composable primitives: the loop is the convenient way to use them, not the only one, and a caller that would rather bound and drive a provider by hand can skip it entirely.\r\n\r\nTools and files are borrowed, not owned. Callable tools come from [`@orkestrel/tool`](tool.md): the loop advertises their definitions to the model, dispatches the calls that come back, and feeds each `ToolResult` in as a tool message. A tool is loop machinery �?\" it is never rendered into the prompt. Documents come from [`@orkestrel/workspace`](workspace.md): the context renders the active workspace into every turn, split by carrier �?\" text as fenced reference blocks in the system message, images attached to the last user turn. That split is this package's own product policy, decided here because only the prompt-assembly layer knows what a turn looks like.\r\n\r\nA turn is bounded and always terminates. One `AbortSignal` �?\" a cancel, a [timeout](timeout.md), and a [budget](budget.md) folded together through `AbortSignal.any` �?\" bounds the whole run, and tool iteration is capped at `limit`. A cancel is not an error: it commits a partial `AgentResult` that resolves, so only a genuine provider or tool failure rejects. `generate` and `stream` share one private run, so the one-shot result can never diverge from the live stream, and a buggy observer cannot corrupt either, because the emitter isolates a listener's throw.\r\n\r\n## Surface\r\n\r\nThe agent-owned surface: the inference boundary and the HTTP engine behind it, the relay that carries that boundary to a browser, the conversation layer, the context and its managers, the loop, the authority gate, and the durable-job bridge. Tool and workspace entities belong to their originating packages and are consumed directly �?\" never re-exported here �?\" and one vendor's wire belongs to the concrete provider that extends `AgentProvider`.\r\n\r\nA provider turns a conversation (plus optional tools) into a turn: `generate` resolves the assembled `ProviderResult` (content + any tool calls + any usage); `stream` yields channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s as they arrive (`content` for answer text, `thinking` for live reasoning) and returns the same assembled result when the stream completes, so a caller can render tokens / reasoning live and still get the full outcome. Both bound the call with an `AbortSignal`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n\r\ndeclare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\r\nconst abort = createAbort()\r\nconst messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\n\r\nconst result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\nresult.content // the assembled content\r\nresult.usage // { prompt, completion, total } �?\" folds into a token budget\r\n\r\nconst generator = provider.stream(messages, abort.signal)\r\nlet step = await generator.next()\r\nwhile (!step.done) {\r\n\tif (step.value.channel === 'content') process.stdout.write(step.value.text)\r\n\tif (step.value.channel === 'thinking') process.stderr.write(step.value.text)\r\n\tstep = await generator.next()\r\n}\r\nconst streamed = step.value // the assembled ProviderResult (content === the joined content deltas)\r\n```\r\n\r\nPass `tools` (a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]`) to advertise callable tools for the turn; when the model calls one, `result.tools` is a `ToolCall[]` (each with a guaranteed `id`, the tool `name`, and parsed `arguments`). Aborting a `stream` mid-flight throws a `ProviderAbortError` whose `partial` holds whatever streamed before the cancel.\r\n\r\nA tool is a JSON-Schema-described callable from [`@orkestrel/tool`](tool.md), and the loop needs exactly this from its registry: `definitions()` advertises the tools to the model, and `execute` dispatches the `ToolCall`s that come back. What returns is a `ToolResult` discriminated on `success` �?\" an unknown name and a throwing handler both arrive as the failure arm rather than as an exception, and a batch isolates each call from its siblings, which is what lets the loop hand every outcome back to the model and let it react:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createTool, createToolManager } from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\n\r\nconst tools = createToolManager()\r\ntools.add([\r\n\tcreateTool({\r\n\t\tname: 'add',\r\n\t\tdescription: 'Add two numbers',\r\n\t\tparameters: { type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'number' }, b: { type: 'number' } } },\r\n\t\texecute: (args) => Number(args.a) + Number(args.b), // narrow the model-supplied unknown\r\n\t}),\r\n\tcreateTool({ name: 'now', execute: () => Date.now() }),\r\n])\r\n\r\nconst definitions = tools.definitions() // hand these to provider.generate / .stream as `tools`\r\nconst results = await tools.execute([\r\n\t{ id: '1', name: 'add', arguments: { a: 2, b: 3 } }, // �+' { success: true, id: '1', name: 'add', value: 5 }\r\n\t{ id: '2', name: 'ghost', arguments: {} }, // �+' { success: false, id: '2', name: 'ghost', error: 'tool not found: ghost' }\r\n])\r\n```\r\n\r\nContained failure is the registry's contract, not a limitation of it: in-process code that wants a typed error calls the tool itself �?\" `tools.tool(name)` then `tool.execute(args)` inside its own `try`/`catch`. Registration, advertising, dispatch, and error containment are documented in [`tool.md`](tool.md).\r\n\r\nCollect a turn's conversation in an `AgentContext`. Add turns through `context.messages` �?\" the active conversation's live tail, always present, satisfying `MessageManagerInterface` by minting each `id` on `add` and keeping stored messages immutable and in insertion order �?\" then `build()` the provider input: `[systemMessage?, ...messages]`. `context.tools` sits beside them, but it is a different kind of thing: the other managers assemble prompt text, while the tool registry exists so the loop can advertise definitions and dispatch calls. Its contents reach the model as the `tools` argument, never as a message:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAgentContext } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport { createToolManager } from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\n\r\ndeclare const provider: ProviderInterface\r\nconst abort = createAbort()\r\nconst context = createAgentContext({ system: 'You are concise.', tools: createToolManager() })\r\ncontext.messages.add([\r\n\t{ role: 'user', content: 'What is 2 + 3?' }, // the `id` is minted by add, not supplied\r\n\t{ role: 'user', content: 'Reply with just the number.' },\r\n])\r\n\r\nconst input = context.build() // [{ role: 'system', content: 'You are concise.' }, �?�the two user turns]\r\nconst definitions = context.tools.definitions() // tools reach the provider here, NOT in `input`\r\nconst result = await provider.generate(input, abort.signal, definitions)\r\n```\r\n\r\n`context.messages.add` mints each message's `id` (a random UUID) and returns the created message(s); `build()` is computed fresh on every call, so it always reflects the current conversation. Without a system prompt, `build()` is only the conversation, and no tool's name, description, or parameter schema ever appears in its output.\r\n\r\nDrive the whole turn with an `Agent` (`createAgent`) �?\" it composes the provider, its `AgentContext`, and the tool registry into the bounded context �+' provider �+' tools �+' repeat loop. Seed the conversation through `agent.context.messages`, then either `generate()` for a one-shot `AgentResult` or `stream()` for a live `AgentChunk` stream (`token` answer deltas, `think` reasoning deltas, `tool` dispatches, `usage`) whose `result` resolves the same `AgentResult`. `generate` drains that same stream, so they can't diverge:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAgent } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createTokenBudget } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\nimport { createTool, createToolManager } from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\n\r\ndeclare const provider: ProviderInterface\r\nconst tools = createToolManager()\r\ntools.add(createTool({ name: 'add', execute: (args) => Number(args.a) + Number(args.b) }))\r\n\r\nconst agent = createAgent(provider, {\r\n\tsystem: 'You are concise.',\r\n\ttools,\r\n\tlimit: 4, // cap tool iterations\r\n\ttimeout: 30_000, // wall-clock deadline for the whole turn\r\n\tbudget: createTokenBudget({ max: 50_000, scope: 'total' }), // cost ceiling\r\n})\r\nagent.context.messages.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Use the add tool to add 2 and 3.' })\r\n\r\nconst stream = agent.stream()\r\nfor await (const chunk of stream.events) {\r\n\tif (chunk.category === 'token') process.stdout.write(chunk.content) // live deltas\r\n\tif (chunk.category === 'think') process.stderr.write(chunk.content) // live reasoning\r\n\tif (chunk.category === 'tool') log(chunk.call, chunk.result) // a dispatched tool + its result\r\n}\r\nconst result = await stream.result // { content, usage?, partial } �?\" usage summed across the turn\r\n```\r\n\r\nBoth `generate` and `stream` accept optional per-run `AgentRunOptions` �?\" `think` and `schema` (forwarded to the provider as `ProviderStreamOptions`) plus `limit` / `timeout` / `budget` / `signal`, each overriding its `AgentOptions` construction default for this run only. Omitting one keeps the constructed default, so a caller that passes no options gets the agent it configured. A per-run `signal` composes with (never replaces) a constructed `signal` �?\" either aborting cancels the run; a per-run `budget` is `start()`ed for that run and is the one the loop charges, leaving a constructed `budget` untouched:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nconst agent = createAgent(provider, { tools, limit: 10, timeout: 60_000 }) // construction defaults\r\nagent.context.messages.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Summarize this doc.' })\r\n\r\n// A tighter, structured-output run -- overrides limit + timeout, adds a schema, for THIS call only.\r\nconst result = await agent.generate({\r\n\tlimit: 2,\r\n\ttimeout: 5_000,\r\n\tschema: { type: 'object', properties: { summary: { type: 'string' } } },\r\n})\r\n```\r\n\r\n`schema`, like `think`, rides into `provider.stream` as a `ProviderStreamOptions`: the loop composes both into one options object, omitting whichever key is unset, and passes no options object at all when neither is present �?\" a provider that never received one still never does.\r\n\r\nThe turn is bounded by one cancel folded from the external `signal` + the `timeout` deadline + the `budget` signal through `AbortSignal.any`; `agent.abort(reason)` (or `stream.abort(reason)`) fires it. A cancel �?\" external, deadline, budget, or `abort()` �?\" commits a partial result: the `result` promise resolves with `{ partial: true, content: <what accumulated> }`, never rejects (a cancel is not an error); only a genuine provider / tool error rejects. An optional `scheduler.yield`s between turns; tool iteration is capped at `limit` (default `DEFAULT_AGENT_LIMIT`). Exhausting `limit` while the model still holds unresolved tool intent (it requested tools on the very last allowed turn) is a distinct, non-cancel cause of `partial: true` �?\" it fires an `exhaust` event (the turns reached) instead of `abort`. A natural finish on the last allowed turn, or `limit: 0` (which never enters the loop), stays `partial: false`. `agent.status` transitions `idle` �+' `running` �+' `done` / `error`.\r\n\r\nGate the model's tool calls with an optional `Authority` (`createAuthority`) �?\" a synchronous policy gate the loop consults before each call runs, passed through `AgentOptions.authority`. It walks ordered `rules` first-match-wins (a matched rule allows unless its `allowed` is `false`), falling back to a configurable default when none match �?\" allow-unmatched by default (a denylist), or deny-by-default when its `fallback` denies (an allowlist). A denied call is never executed: the loop synthesizes the failure arm of `ToolResult` (`error: 'denied: <reason>'`) and feeds it back as a `tool` chunk and a tool message, so no handler runs, no budget is spent, and the model still sees what happened and can choose something else. An allowed call dispatches normally, and with no `authority` set every call dispatches:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAgent, createAuthority } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createTool, createToolManager } from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\n\r\nconst tools = createToolManager()\r\ntools.add([\r\n\tcreateTool({ name: 'add', execute: (args) => Number(args.a) + Number(args.b) }),\r\n\tcreateTool({ name: 'delete', execute: (args) => drop(args.id) }),\r\n])\r\n\r\n// A denylist: deny `delete`, allow everything else (the default allow fallback).\r\nconst authority = createAuthority({\r\n\trules: [\r\n\t\t{\r\n\t\t\tmatch: (c) => c.call.name === 'delete',\r\n\t\t\tzone: 'restricted',\r\n\t\t\tallowed: false,\r\n\t\t\treason: 'read-only mode',\r\n\t\t},\r\n\t],\r\n})\r\nconst agent = createAgent(provider, { tools, authority })\r\nagent.context.messages.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Delete record 42.' })\r\n// When the model calls `delete`, the loop feeds back { error: 'denied: read-only mode' } �?\" never runs it.\r\n```\r\n\r\nAlongside the conversation store sits the standalone `InstructionManager` a richer context assembles a prompt from �?\" named directives, keyed by `name`, listed by descending `priority`. It mirrors the registry shape �?\" `add` (one or a batch) mints each `id` and overwrites a same-key entry (last write wins), an `instruction(name)` / `instructions()` accessor pair, `remove` (one or a batch) / `clear` / `count` �?\" holds immutable entries, and is observable (`emitter` with an `add` / `remove` / `clear` event map, wired through the reserved `on` option; an `error` option receives a listener's throw). It carries the **build-contract** members a context's assembly step calls: `open` (the section header text, `'## Instructions'`) and `render(instruction)` (per-item rendering �?\" the instruction's `content`):\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createInstructionManager } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n\r\nconst instructions = createInstructionManager()\r\nconst safety = instructions.add({\r\n\tname: 'safety',\r\n\tcontent: 'Refuse unsafe requests.',\r\n\tpriority: 10,\r\n})\r\ninstructions.open // '## Instructions'\r\ninstructions.render(safety) // 'Refuse unsafe requests.'\r\n```\r\n\r\nDocuments reach a turn one way only: through the active workspace. A [`@orkestrel/workspace`](workspace.md) workspace is a flat map of immutable files, and it takes no position on prompts �?\" deciding how a file becomes part of a turn is this package's job, and the decision is a split by carrier. A text file renders as a fenced reference block in a `## Workspace` system section, where the model can read it as quoted material. An image file cannot be text, so its `base64` payload attaches to the last user message instead, which is where a vision model looks. A message carries that payload on its optional `images` field �?\" `Message` and `MessageInput` both accept `readonly images?: readonly string[]` �?\" and a vision-capable provider forwards it onto the wire (an empty or absent array is never sent). It is input-only; `ProviderResult` is unchanged.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n\r\ndeclare const provider: ProviderInterface // a vision-capable model\r\nconst result = await provider.generate(\r\n\t[{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Describe this image.', images: ['<payload>'] }],\r\n\tabort.signal,\r\n)\r\n```\r\n\r\n`AgentContext` wires the instruction manager and the workspace registry in. Beyond `system`, `messages`, and `tools`, a context exposes its own `instructions` manager and `workspaces` registry �?\" pass pre-built ones through `AgentContextOptions`, or fresh empty ones are created �?\" and `build()` folds them into the turn. The assembly order is one leading `system` message holding the system prompt, then the non-empty instructions block (its `description` header followed by every item's `format`), then the active workspace's text files under a `## Workspace` header, joined by blank lines; then the conversation. With no instructions, no active workspace, and no scope, that reduces to exactly the lean `[systemMessage?, ...messages]`. The carrier split shows here: text rides the system block, image data rides the last user message.\r\n\r\n````ts\r\nimport { createAgentContext } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n\r\nconst context = createAgentContext({ system: 'You are a code reviewer.' })\r\ncontext.instructions.add({ name: 'tone', content: 'Be terse.', priority: 10 })\r\ncontext.workspaces.add().write('src/main.ts', 'export const x = 1') // the active workspace\r\ncontext.messages.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Review this.' })\r\n\r\nconst input = context.build()\r\n// input[0] = { role: 'system', content:\r\n//   'You are a code reviewer.\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nBe terse.\\n\\n## Workspace\\n\\nFile: src/main.ts\\n```typescript\\nexport const x = 1\\n```' }\r\n// input[1] = { role: 'user', content: 'Review this.' }\r\n````\r\n\r\n### Conversations & compaction\r\n\r\nAbove the flat `MessageManagerInterface` sits the `Conversation` (`createConversation` / a `ConversationManager`) �?\" it owns its messages directly and compacts older ones into summarized `sections` so a long history fits a turn's context window without discarding the originals. Append turns through the conversation's own `add` (the live uncompacted tail; `message` / `messages` / `remove` / `clear` / `count` round it out); `compact()` folds the older live messages into a summarized `Section` (retaining their originals), regenerates the conversation rollup `summary`, and shrinks `view()` �?\" the model input, where each section becomes one summary message followed by the live tail. Compaction is driven by a provider-agnostic `ConversationSummaryHandler` seam (`(messages) => Promise<string>`) the agent runtime supplies, so a `compact()` without one throws a `ConversationError`. `keep` retains a recent tail (default `DEFAULT_CONVERSATION_KEEP` = `0`, fold all); `rehydrate(id)` / `search(query)` read the retained originals:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createConversation } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n\r\ndeclare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\r\n// The summarizer seam �?\" built from the provider by the runtime; core stays provider-agnostic.\r\n// Append the instruction as the FINAL user turn: a chat model emits nothing when the prompt\r\n// ends on an assistant turn, so a leading-system instruction is unreliable.\r\nconst conversation = createConversation({\r\n\tsummarize: async (messages) =>\r\n\t\t(\r\n\t\t\tawait provider.generate(\r\n\t\t\t\t[\r\n\t\t\t\t\t...messages,\r\n\t\t\t\t\t{ id: 's', role: 'user', content: 'Summarize the conversation so far concisely.' },\r\n\t\t\t\t],\r\n\t\t\t\tAbortSignal.timeout(30_000),\r\n\t\t\t)\r\n\t\t).content,\r\n\tkeep: 2, // retain the two most recent turns verbatim on each compaction\r\n})\r\nconversation.add([\r\n\t{ role: 'user', content: 'My name is Ada.' },\r\n\t{ role: 'assistant', content: 'Nice to meet you, Ada.' },\r\n\t{ role: 'user', content: 'What did I say my name was?' },\r\n])\r\n\r\nconst section = await conversation.compact() // folds the older turns �+' a summarized section\r\nconversation.view() // [<section summary message>, ...the retained recent tail] �?\" the model input\r\nconversation.summary // the regenerated rollup (a summary-of-summaries over all sections)\r\nconversation.search('ada') // case-insensitive across sections' originals + the live tail\r\nsection && conversation.rehydrate(section.id) // the section's full original messages (a pure read)\r\n```\r\n\r\nRegister a conversation in a `ConversationManager` and pass that registry through `AgentContextOptions.conversations` to make it the message source. `context.messages` then is the active conversation's live tail, and `build()` folds its `view()`; the conversation owns message inclusion through compaction, which is why a scope never filters messages. When the registry is omitted, the context creates one with an active default conversation.\r\n\r\nPass that registry through `AgentOptions.conversations` together with an `AgentOptions.window` context [`Budget`](budget.md) to enable automatic compaction. Each turn the loop estimates the current full prompt against the window and, when it reaches the ceiling, compacts the active summarizable conversation before continuing on the rebuilt view. Omit `window` and the loop does not auto-compact. See the [Contract](#contract) (the automatic-compaction clause) for the exact trigger and single-level limitation.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAgent, createConversationManager, estimateMessages } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createBudget } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\n\r\nconst conversations = createConversationManager({ summarize, keep: 2 })\r\n{\r\n\t\"name\": \"@orkestrel/agent\",\r\n\t\"version\": \"0.0.21\",\r\n\t\"description\": \"A typed conversation runtime for the @orkestrel line �?\" providers, conversations, prompt assembly, authority, and the bounded agent loop. Because it is optional, no provider is forced to supply one �?\" omitting it leaves every section on the managers' built-in framing.\r\n378: \r\n379: ### The HTTP provider engine\r\n380: \r\n381: `ProviderInterface` is the boundary; `AgentProvider` is the engine behind it. Every HTTP-backed provider needs the same machinery �?\" a deadline, a transport, an authorization hook, a bounded error read, a chunk decoder, reasoning separation, and result assembly �?\" and rewriting that per vendor is how a fleet of providers drifts apart. `AgentProvider` owns all of it and leaves exactly one thing open: the wire. A subclass supplies `name` and the wire seams �?\" `frame()` returns fresh framing state for the call, `body(request)` projects a `ProviderRequest` onto the vendor's request shape, `read(record)` decodes one framed record into a `ProviderIncrement`, and `finish(parser)` returns whatever the parser still held at end of input. The engine is generic over `TRecord`: the record type a call's `frame()` parser emits and `read` consumes, defaulting to `Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>` for a wire whose records are JSON objects. The constructor switches decide the rest: `split` separates in-content `<think>` reasoning from the answer (default `true`), and `strict` requires the wire to carry a settled `result` record rather than assembling one at end of input (default `false`).\r\n382: \r\n383: ```ts\r\n384: import type { AgentProviderInput, ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n385: import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\n386: \r\n387: declare function createTextProvider(options: AgentProviderInput): ProviderInterface\r\n388: declare function token(signal: AbortSignal): Promise<string>\r\n389: const abort = createAbort()\r\n390: const messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\n391: \r\n392: const provider = createTextProvider({\r\n393: \turl: 'https://api.example',\r\n394: \tpath: '/generate', // appended to `url` on every call\r\n395: \ttimeout: 30_000, // the base's own deadline; DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT when omitted\r\n396: \theaders: async (signal) => ({ authorization: await token(signal) }), // awaited inside that deadline\r\n397: \tsplit: true, // route <think> spans to `thinking`, yield the clean answer\r\n398: \tstrict: false, // assemble at end of input instead of requiring a settled record\r\n399: })\r\n400: const result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\n401: result.content // the assembled answer, with any <think> span routed to result.thinking\r\n402: ```\r\n403: \r\n404: `AgentProvider` is `abstract`; the members a subclass fills are listed under [`## Methods`](#methods).\r\n405: \r\n406: The engine's failures arrive as one error class. A `ProviderError` carries a machine-readable `code` and, for an HTTP failure alone, the response `status`; `isProviderError` narrows a caught value so a caller can branch on the code:\r\n407: \r\n408: - `'HTTP'` �?\" a non-OK response. The message is `provider error: <status>`, with ` - <excerpt>` appended only when the response body carried text. The excerpt is read to at most `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` bytes and the remainder of the body is cancelled, so a stalled error body cannot hold the call open past its deadline.\r\n409: - `'PROTOCOL'` �?\" a successful response with no body, a wire record the concrete provider refuses, or a `strict` stream that ended without a settled result.\r\n410: - `'PROVIDER'` �?\" an upstream failure a relay reported in an `error` frame.\r\n411: \r\n412: A cancel is not in that taxonomy: the call's own deadline and the caller's signal are folded into one bound, and a cancel throws `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial assembled so far.\r\n413: \r\n414: ### The relay\r\n415: \r\n416: A browser must not hold a model credential, so this package ships the hop rather than the credential. `createRelay` returns a `RelayHandler` �?\" a plain `(request: Request) => Promise<Response>` you mount on any fetch-standard router �?\" that authorizes the request, validates its JSON body against `providerRequestContract`, and streams one upstream `provider.stream` call back as newline-delimited `RelayFrame` records under `RELAY_CONTENT_TYPE`. `createRelayProvider` is the other end: a `ProviderInterface` the browser drives exactly like a local one, which posts the request and decodes those frames back into deltas and the settled result. `RelayStream` is the response half `createRelay` composes, exported for a host that mounts its own route.\r\n417: \r\n418: ```ts\r\n419: import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n420: import { createRelay, createRelayProvider } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n421: // The browser application supplies this parser dependency.\r\n422: import { createNDJSONParser } from '@orkestrel/ndjson'\r\n423: \r\n424: declare const upstream: ProviderInterface // the server-side provider holding the credential\r\n425: \r\n426: // On the server: the route, the authorization decision, and the byte budget.\r\n427: const handler = createRelay({\r\n428: \tprovider: upstream,\r\n429: \tauthorize: (request) => request.headers.get('authorization') === 'Bearer session-token',\r\n430: \tlimit: 65_536, // a body at or above this answers 413\r\n431: })\r\n432: \r\n433: // In the browser: a provider like any other, reached over that route.\r\n434: const browser = createRelayProvider({\r\n435: \turl: 'https://app.example/relay',\r\n436: \tparser: createNDJSONParser,\r\n437: \theaders: () => ({ authorization: 'Bearer session-token' }),\r\n438: })\r\n439: ```\r\n440: \r\n441: The frame vocabulary is `RelayFrame`: a `ProviderDelta` (`content` or `thinking`) per streamed delta, one `{ channel: 'result', result }` when the turn settles, `{ channel: 'abort', partial }` when the upstream call was cancelled, and `{ channel: 'error', message }` for anything else �?\" always the fixed `RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE` text, so an upstream failure's own message never reaches the browser. A refusal never becomes a frame: the handler answers `401` when `authorize` refuses or throws, `400` when the body is missing, unreadable, or rejected by the contract, `413` when the body reaches the byte limit, and `502` when the upstream call cannot be constructed. Each refusal carries no body and reaches the browser as a `ProviderError` with the `HTTP` code and that status.\r\n442: \r\n443: The wire is strictly narrower than the domain on purpose. `providerRequestContract` and `relayFrameContract` are compiled from the shapes in [Shapes and contracts](#shapes-and-contracts), and `RelayProvider.body` refuses a request the JSON wire cannot carry �?\" a function-valued tool argument, a parameter schema holding one �?\" before it fetches. The body it sends is an owned snapshot of that projection read through property descriptors, so a serializer reachable only through a `get` trap or a prototype is never consulted; an own function-valued property such as a `toJSON` method is a value outside JSON and is refused before fetching, with the clone's failure as the refusal's `cause`. A `ToolCall.caller` is local context and never crosses the hop.\r\n444: \r\n445: ### Factories\r\n446: \r\n447: | API                               | Kind     | Summary
